Bishop f3 cannot reach h6 (it's on f3, h6 is a dark square? f3 is a dark square. f3 to h5 is a diagonal. f3 to g4 to h5. Wait, f3 is dark. h6 is light? f3(dark) -> g4(light) -> h5(dark). No, f3 is dark, g4 is light, h5 is dark, g2 is light, h1 is dark. So f3 controls g4, h5 and e4, d5, c6, b7, a8. Wait, Bishop f3 controls the a8-h1 diagonal? No, f3 is a dark square. The diagonal is h1-a8. So f3 controls g2, e4, d5, c6, b7, a8.
What about the other diagonal? f3 is on d1-h5. So it controls e2, g4, h5.
So h6 is NOT attacked by the Bishop at f3.
Is h6 attacked by the Rook at a8? No (rank 8).
Is h6 attacked by the Rook at e7? No (rank 7).
Is h6 attacked by the Knight at h2? Knight at h2 attacks f1, f3, g4. Not h6.
So h6 is safe.

What about h7g6?
If Kh7 moves to g6:
Is g6 attacked?
Rooks are on ranks 8 and 7.
Bishop f3 controls the h5-d1 diagonal. g4 is on it. g6 is not.
Wait, Bishop f3 is on a dark square. g6 is a light square. So Bishop cannot attack g6.
Knight h2 attacks f1, f3, g4. Not g6.
So g6 is also safe.

Between h6 and g6, which is better?
In this weird position, the Black King is alone. There are no other black pieces.
The goal is to survive or draw.
Moving to h6 keeps the king on the edge.
Moving to g6 brings the king slightly more central but might be more exposed to future checks.
However, we only have two legal moves.
Let's look at the board again.
White pieces: Ra8, Re7, Pf4, Pc3, Bf3, Nh2, Kb1.
Black: Kh7.
White has a massive advantage. Black is just trying to survive.
If Black moves Kh7-h6:
White might play Re7-e6+ or something.
If Black moves Kh7-g6:
White might play Re7-e6+ check again.

Wait, if Kh7-h6, White can play Re7-e6+.
If Kh7-g6, White can play Re7-e6+? No, e6 is not on the same rank as g6.
If Kh7-g6, White can play Re7-g7+? Yes, the rook can move from e7 to g7.
Wait, if Kh7 moves to g6, the Rook at e7 is now at the same rank (7) but the king is at rank 6.
So the Rook can move to g7 and check the king again? Yes, Rg7+.
Then the King must move again.

If Kh7 moves to h6, and White plays Re7-h7+? No, the king is at h6. Re7-h7+ doesn't work.
White could play Re7-e6+? Yes. Then the king must move.

Basically, Black is in a desperate situation.
Let's just pick one. h6 usually keeps the king a bit more tucked away.

From g6, the king can move to f5? No, pawn at f4.
From g6, the king can move to h5? No, Bishop f3 controls h5.
From g6, the king can move to f7? No, Rook e7 controls rank 7.
From g6, the king can move to h7? Recapture? No.

From h6, the king can move to g5? Maybe.
From h6, the king can move to h5? No, Bishop f3 controls h5.
From h6, the king can move to g7? No, Rook e7.
